                    Unidentified Flying Object Observed,
           ~~"      Richmond, B·C·   79-11- 29
                                                                                                                                '
                                                                                                                                                   '   .
                Cpl . LUCAS of Richmond Detachment investigated an Unidentified· Object
                observed at Richmond. on the 79-11-29 at approx. 0200 Hrs. Pacif~c
                Standard Tiae an Unidentified Flying Object was seen by susan VAN DEN
                BOOGAARD , (B t 61-11-Q2), of 8591 Francis Road, Richmond, a.o . It ·
                was a clear night. She was at her home and ob~erved the unidentified
                Fl ying Object travelling ,west to east over Northern Richmond. She
                described ita shape as having the appearapce of an upside down saucer
                with a grfJY top. The bottom was .flat and dull gold in colour with a
                black center which appeared to protrude slightly. There ware four (4)
                lights evenly spaced on the bottom near the circumference· and also
                what appeared to be triangular ~ped vents. The top appe~ed to be
                spinning while the bottom was motionless. It made a hUIIIIIling noise        •
                quite loud. She observed 'the u.F . O. for approx. 2 mine. from a •          •
                reasonably short distance at which time she went to get her parents.
                She returned approx. :(/2 adns. later at which time it was disappearing
                t.o the east. . There was no other known observant . susan VAti DEN
               ,BOOOAARD i~ 19 yrs. ' old, s he lives wj.th her parents and works at
                vancouver Int 'l Airport ·as an Insurance Sales parson. She seems
                intelligen~ and reliable and advises she had not been drinking prior
                to seeing 1{he U. F. O. She did not appear to be the type of person who would
                imagine spotting a U. F. O. or mald.ng a .false report. As she works at
                the Airport · she is familiar with the appearance of aircraft& at nights .
                Details of the sighting were forWarded to the Area control centre at'
                vancouver Int ' l Airport at tqe time of , it being reported.         ~,

             ....,                 RE: O.F.O.   SIGRTIN~S

                            This correspondence is in furtherance to our telex of the
                  3rd instant covering an alleged U.F.O. sighting by one Gail DACEY. ·
                           Please be advised that at ~758 'hrs . 79 DEC 02, this office
                 received a phone call from Gail (Mrs. Wayne) DACEY (DB: 08 MAY 48)
                 of the NAPLES district, Alberta. NAP~ES is located some 20 miles
                 northwest of WESTLOCK, Alberta . Cat. D. R. McCORRISTON of this office
                 subsequently attended at the DACEY farm, however, no evidence could
                 be found whip~ might remotely be connected with the apparition. The
                 sighting and subsequent investigation occurred after sunset.
                              On 79 DEC 03, ~pl . R.W. MOW, also of this office, attended
                 at the DACEY farm in response to information received fro~ Mrs . DACEY
                 to t .h e effect that imprints of · unknown origin had been found in the
                 snow at or near the locatio~ of the sighting. Cpl. HOW'S examination
                 of the imprints established that same were created by a young child
                  (or children), prob~ly DACEY ' S off-spring~ and definitely not connected
                 ~n any way with the alleged sighting.       The imprints were approximately
                 75 - ~00 feet away from where the apparition was sighted .
                                                                                                                      \
                             From investigations conducted by Cpl. MQW and Cs~. McCORRISTON,
                 the followin~ circumstances are provided . At approximate~y 1730 hrs.
                 79 DEC ' 02, DACEX ' S Doberman Pincher dog began· barking at the rear
                 door (outside) .of the DACEY residence. Mrs. DACEY" responded to the
                 back door to ~nvestigate what was causing the dog to become aroused .
                 At the back door , she found the dog facing a northwest ~irectiort from
                 the house, looking in that direction . She observed a bright blue
                 object glowing some 300 feet to the northwest. Mrs. DACEY walked
                 approximately 25 feet from the house in an effort to better identify
                 what she was looking at, then apparently became frightened and returned
                 to the house Where she at~empted to telephone her husband at work.
                 Between other ~elephone calla made, she had occasion to observe the
                 object over a period of approximately 15 minutes . The object was
                 initially noted to be a very bright blue color hQWever 1 as the
                 obaer•~tion continped,, tne color dimmed ~n intensity.      The object
                 was football shaped with a length estimated at 5 feet . According to
                 Mrs. DACEY, the length was vertical to the ground . No sound cou~d be
        '        heard aminating from ~he object, whJoh remained suspended approximately
                 20 feet ~ve the ground, probably due, according to Mrs. DACEY, to
    •            the dog ' s continuous barking. Mrs. DACEY ' S two son's, ~aUl (12 yra.)
                 and David (9 yrs.) apparently also observed the apparition •

              In the early morning of October 5/1979 I was worki.n g the
              nigbt shift at the Amerada gas plant approx. twelve miles
              north ·of Rocky. The aky was clear, except for a few       ,

        Cat . L.M. LEYB 131349 - Fireball Sighting
        Hope, B.C.                        79-01-31
        (HOPB DETACHMENT CASB)             (UNORG)
                             '                                         •
                         •
l.   On 79-01-31 at 17r20 bra. above noted member sighted a fireball
while off, s nift In Hope Detachment Area. The weath~r conditions at
the time of sighting were clear just before dusk. ~h~ location of
the above noted member was on 3rd Avenue in front
                                                -· of
                                                    . the Overwaitea
Grocery Store at Hope, B.C .
2.   The color of the fireball was white to yellow with a white trail.
It was located in the Northern sky to the east of ..Dog Mountain and was
the size of a large star. The duration was approximately 2 seconds in
length with no Aound being emitted from the fireball. T~ere were no
other occurrences af'ter the burnup of the fireball in the sky.

    as per Operational Manual 1 . 3. E.22 . , This date at approx. 2210 Hrs Mr.
    PELLETIER contacted Regina Det . R. C.M. Pollc& advising there was an Wll.u:n~lll!l
    object with red lights over a field approx. 3 kms west of his residenc&.
    The object had been observed for approximately 1 h~ur before disap~~at
    to the west . Members on duty at the time were on other matters          un-
    fortunately were unable to attend. the scene . The Regina Airport Tower
    was contacted and did advise there was no aircraft in the vicinity.

    2.     This date writer contacted Mr. PELLETIER and obtai.iled the ... v .......,,""'''l
    information . The sighting was observed by PELLE'l'IER, his wife Patrtcia
    PELLETIER (Age 26) and 3 others visiting with the PELLETIERS . PELLETIER
    observed the object from his residence which is located in north west
•   at 7307 Bennett Drive . The sighting occurred approx. 3 kms ~est , over a
    field, between 2130 Hrs and 2230 Hrs c . s .T. on 80 FEB 25 . · The sky as
    described by PELLETIER was clear and stars were visible . Three gther ,
    persons who witnessed the .object are as follows :
            1j Sidney PELLETIER - Age 28 - Regina, S~ek .

    of PELLET(ERS ' interview, thus comp ete information on them could not be
    obtained .

    3.      The object observed by PELLETIER is described as a circular bright
    light . The color was white changing to green t.hen to red and repeating
    approx . every 30 seconds. PELLETIER estimated the altitude to be approx.
    650 feet and was observing the object from approx. 3 kms . away . No move-
    ment observed for approx. 1 hour then the object went straight west away
    from viewer until it got smaller before it disappeared . PELLETIER stated
    only ~ object was viewed a~d d~ration lasted approx. l hour.

.

          utes then decided to contact t he R. C.M. P. who advised that they

               Dear Sirs:
               1.       This letter 1s being forwarded to you people as per our
               lnstructional 'Operational M.a ,nual l.S,E.22.a. We have had reported to
               our office two separate sigbtings of 1 UNIDENTIFI~ FLYING OBJECTS.     •
               2.       · Mr. Marti~ J. VAN ·MET~E sigh ted a single bril{ht white light
               at 2130 ~rs., ~6FEB80, 7 miles Bast, 4 miles North and 4 miles East
               of Lloydminster, SaSkatCHewan. VAN METRE explained that this objec~
               was at the right of him at a distande of apprpximately 100 yards.
               As be drove East on the grid road, this object stayed parallel to hiM
               as be proceeded to drive up a large hill and stopped his truck.
               VAN NETRE turned his en~ine ott, stepped out from his truck, and observed
               t his object- for a •few minutes. The object made no sound and was
               approximately ~ mile ott the earth surface. VAN ~ETRE started his
               vehicle up, proceeded down the road for t mile and the object dis-
               appeared. There was no road, trail, or tracks, etc. to the righ~
               of tbis irid road tor 2 miles. Tbe land surface is obstructed with
               bills and hollows.
               3.        The second sighting was ort 28FEB80 at 2025 hrs. At the same
               location, VAN METRE, his brother Ben~ arid a good triend--Charlie
               WALDERN saw a single bright light approximately ~ mile above the earth
               travelling West. This object made no sound and did not l~av e any
               t rail of light or debris .
                   •
          ' 4.       VAN METRE reported the second sighting and Cst. ~fELENCROK
            was dispatched to the above noted location, When driving t9 the scene,
            this object could be seen from Lloydminster, S•skatchewan . Upon
        \ arriving at tbe scene, the object had moved completely from the East
            sky to tbe West. I was standing on the road talking to' the pther t hree
,           gentlemen and t his object disappeared.

                             Dear Sirs:
                                       The following fs a report of an unidentified 1
                             flying object which was sighted on J O ~R 16 in the Fort
                             Pitt District, Saskatchewan. Don s~        of X707 - 46bh
                             Ave ., Lloydminster, Canada was the person who reported
                             the sighting. The object was first seen in the northern
                             sky at approximately 2130 .hrs ., M. S.T. The object was
                             ~ oval shaped bright 11f,ht "{i.th a long upside "V" coming
                             out of the bottom. The 'V" was qlue in colour. There
                             was also a small red light on the le.ft side of the "V".
                             ~be object moved to the north west and seemed to hover
                             in that position. The object was visible for approximate- ,
                             ly two hours.          ·
                                                                  ,       I                   '
                                      The object 1was also seen by Me . Shelly WEBER
                             of Lloydmihetar, Canada. Her story was identical to that
                             of SPENCE ( she was with SPENCE) .                  •
                                      This Detachment has not been able to determine
                             what this obj~pt was and bae 1expired all a~enuee of
                             investigation open to us .   ,

             ~~                     Dear Sir :
     ~A)                            I just spoke to Professor Crawford of McGill University in
·~\ · 6v                            Montreal , who answers questions the public may have concerning
J
         . \A
      \ ••

               ~
               ~
                                    astronomy . He suggested that I write to you .
                                      at night , at 8 p . m. (perhaps a minute or two before or a minute
                                    after 8 p . m. ), I was1 walking along Maisonneuve Blvd . in Montreal ,
             ...                    near Guy Street . I· looked at the sky in an easternly direction
                                    when I noticed a large extremely bright round object falling down
              •                     in a steep curve , with a tail that was leas bright , ~d perhaps
                                    10 td 1 ~ times as long as the diameter of the falling object .
                                    It all happened so quickly , perhaps two seconds , apd the object
                                    was lost from view behind some skyscrapers in the city .
 •                                  It is , of course , impossible for me to say how big the object
                                    could, have been or how far away . I will make t wo drawings at
                                    the end of my letter to give you a further idea of m~ observation .
        '                            Professor Crawford ~bought that it may have been ~meteor and he
                                     said that very bright meteors are seen only a few times during
                                     the year . And only a few people ever see it , because few people
                                     look at the sky , and it has to be at that precise moment , because .
                                     a second or two the meteor is gone , unless one is in the country
                                     and oBh see it just a little bit longer . Professor Crawford
                                   • also thought that you may be interested in this information for
                                     your r eoorde/ statistios .    1

             While traveling north on MacKenzie Highway on April 17, 1980
  I observed an object in the sky east of the road and sli~t]Jr ahead
  of the car. It was approximate]Jr 4:55 in the morning and•lthe sun was
  coming up bUt still behind the trees . There was some cloud in the s~
  on the northern horizon and some orange/pink ,cloud on the eastern
 ~orizon . The object was pointed out to me by my wi!e and at first it
  looked l ike a small white cloud that was reflecting the bright l silvery
  Sun.shine on it . At first it appeared to be stationary • and•
                                                                  by this
        •              •
1 t1m.e we had turned a few corners and the object was now on the east

  side of the car.' The object thttn appeared. to ' move , parallel to the
  highway traveling north and gainjng some altitude then disappeared
 behind a large cloud. It re -appe~ed above the cloud. still traveling
  northern]Jr and I stopped the car to take a picture of it. After
  mounting my 2X multipiier and 4o5 mm telephoto lens on the c~ra I
  cou1d not take a picture as the film was on '5 on the camera . • The •
  object by this time was tra~eling at a high rate ~f speed and at a
  northwesterly dir~tion. I would estimate that the object was four
  times oits height in length. The entire siting lasted about 2 minutes.
 The object . was at least 1000 feet in altitude and approximately a half
                                                                0

 mile away. During the siting the diecussion of -the obj'ec:t in the car
